- FIX 1 (L1-D P1 — capability-scene.tsx, THE key fix): deleted the dead parallax plumbing — `const mouse = useRef({tx,ty})` (was :306) and the whole `e.currentTarget.getBoundingClientRect?.()` block (was :462-466) that could never run under R3F v9's event-hijack shim (no getBoundingClientRect on the pointer-capture proxy). The useFrame parallax (now :444-446) reads R3F's maintained NDC pointer directly: `const tx = (state.pointer.x * viewport.width) / 4` / same for ty — the file's own ÷4 amplitude and 0.04 easing preserved, still lerping the `parallax` parent group so blob+satellites+halo drift together. state.pointer updates on every canvas pointermove (R3F event pass), so no listener and no rect read remain. Grep-verified: zero remaining consumers of the deleted mouse ref.
- FIX 2 (L1-C/L1-D P2 — console-scene.tsx, drag was promised but never existed): implemented the clamped drag by mirroring capability-scene's proven throttled pattern at the DOM wrapper level — ConsoleScene now returns a wrapper div (`absolute inset-0`) with onPointerDown (setDragging + record start + `e.currentTarget.setPointerCapture?.(e.pointerId)`), onPointerMove (FIX(2-c/4) ~60Hz timestamp throttle, early-return BEFORE updating `last` so deltas accumulate correctly; yaw free `+= dx*0.008`, pitch `THREE.MathUtils.clamp(rotation.x + dy*0.008, ±MAX_TILT)` with new `const MAX_TILT = 0.5` ≈ ±29° — the "clamped" promise, console can never flip upside down), onPointerUp/onPointerCancel (setDragging(false) + guarded `hasPointerCapture`/`releasePointerCapture`). Wrapper-level DOM handlers chosen over R3F group handlers so the WHOLE scene box is a drag surface (R3F raycast events would only START a drag on a node/sprite hit; this scene's ray targets are sparse — presses on empty space would stay dead). The group mutation channel: Nodes registers its rotation group UP to ConsoleScene via a `registerGroup` callback prop (see react-compiler note below); the existing useFrame auto-drift pauses while dragging (capability-consistent) and its drift tilt is now clamped to the same ±MAX_TILT so drift alone can never flip the console either (was unbounded += 0.02/s → upside-down after ~2.6min). Canvas cursor:'grab' KEPT — moved to the wrapper as `dragging ? 'grabbing' : 'grab'` (now truthful); Canvas keeps `style={{position:'absolute', inset:0}}` minus the cursor. Added ContextLossGuard (mirror of hero-canvas:531 / capability-scene:541 — preventDefault + one-line warn) inside the Canvas (L1-C P2 parity). Header comment rewritten to describe what's actually implemented.
- FIX 3 (L1-C/L1-D P3 — WebGL gate + truthful rotate cursor): NEW src/lib/use-webgl.ts — `probeWebGL()` (synchronous one-shot, memoized at module level so all consumers share ONE context-creation probe; `createElement('canvas').getContext('webgl2') ?? getContext('webgl')` in try/catch; server → false WITHOUT pinning the cache) + `useWebGLSupport()` hook (rAF-deferred exactly like hero-canvas's glAvailable pattern — no synchronous context creation in the effect body; SSR-safe false start). hero-console.tsx:197 gate strengthened `!('WebGLRenderingContext' in window)` → `!webglSupported` (from the new hook) — driver-blocklisted browsers now get the SVG diagram instead of R3F's un-awaited async configure() rejection → silent empty 280px box. Cursor wiring: removed the dead `data-cursor="rotate" data-cursor-label={t('placeholder')}` attrs from the pointer-events-none label chip (kept the chip as the visible preset-name hint) and put `data-cursor={showSvg ? undefined : 'rotate'}` on the hit-testable `#hero-console-scene` wrapper that now receives the drag. DEVIATION (documented): dropped the explicit data-cursor-label={t('placeholder')} when moving the affordance — t('placeholder') is the search-input placeholder ("صف مشروعك بكلمة واحدة…"), NOT a rotate label; making it live would have mislabeled the cursor ring. With only data-cursor="rotate" present, the custom-cursor engine resolves the label from its own common.cursor.rotate context map = "اسحب للتدوير 360°" / "Drag to rotate 360°" — the exact text the audit quoted as the promised affordance (same mechanism three-d-section already relies on).
- FIX 4 (L1-D P2+P3 — hero-canvas.tsx mobile tier + ultrawide): `PARTICLE_COUNT = 4500` → `PARTICLE_COUNT_DESKTOP = 4500` / `PARTICLE_COUNT_MOBILE = 1800`. New `useMobileTier()` hook — the EXACT useSyncExternalStore pattern of use-reduced-motion.ts: module-level MQL singleton for the combined query `'(max-width: 767px), (pointer: coarse)'` (comma = OR → coarse pointer OR narrow viewport), subscribe/getSnapshot/getServerSnapshot (false on server — HeroCanvas is a client-only dynamic import anyway). Reactive WITHOUT canvas re-creation: tier flip swaps `dpr={[1,1.5]}` ↔ `dpr={[1,2]}` (R3F re-applies the pixel ratio in place, no GL context recreation) and remounts `<Particles key={particleCount} count={particleCount}>` so the lazy-useState buffers resize (buffers are sized once per mount by design — Math.random purity); the Canvas/GL context itself survives. Particles signature `{mouse}` → `{mouse, count}`; desktop path byte-identical (4500 + dpr [1,2]). Silk plane `args={[64, 32]}` → `args={[80, 32]}` (covers 32:9 ultrawides: visible half-width at the dolly's farthest z ≈ 39.4 < 40; cost-free for a 1-segment fullscreen quad; height/fov untouched so the ±10.5 vertical melt math is unchanged).
- FIX 5 (L1-D P3 — three-d-section.tsx accessible interactive visual): wrapper gained `role="img"` (aria-label was on a role-less div — ignored by most AT) + `tabIndex={!reduced ? 0 : undefined}` + focus ring mirroring the hero CTA pattern (`focus-visible:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-ring focus-visible:ring-offset-2 focus-visible:ring-offset-elyra-dark` — compiles, unlike the old ring-offset-elyra-deep). Keyboard path: onKeyDown (ArrowLeft/Right/Up/Down → `sceneRef.current?.nudge(±16, 0)` / `(0, ±16)`; 16 "drag pixels" ≈ 0.16 rad ≈ 9° per press; preventDefault so the page doesn't scroll while rotating; guard no-ops before the lazy scene mounts). The nudge channel: capability-scene exports `CapabilitySceneHandle {nudge(dx,dy)}` and accepts `ref` via React 19 ref-as-prop + useImperativeHandle — verified END-TO-END against installed sources that the ref survives next/dynamic → React.lazy (next 16.1.3 loadable.js spreads all props incl. ref onto React.lazy; react-dom 19.2.3 jsx-runtime keeps ref IN props, tag-16 lazy beginWork passes pendingProps — ref included — to the resolved function component; @types/react 19.2.8 documents the `ref?: Ref<T>` in-props pattern). three-d-section imports the handle TYPE-ONLY (`import type { CapabilitySceneHandle }`) so the lazy chunk boundary is untouched. nudge applies the SAME ±0.01 rad/px mapping as the pointer drag to the SAME spinner group (blob+satellites+rings rotate identically to an equivalent drag).
- react-compiler discipline (found empirically, 0-warnings requirement): first pass passed refs as props (spinnerRef/groupRef) → react-compiler warned "Mutating component props" on the useFrame drift lines (it cannot prove a prop is a ref object). Redesign: each component mutates ONLY refs it created locally — Centerpiece/Nodes keep their own group ref for drag+drift, and publish the group UP via a `registerSpinner`/`registerGroup` useCallback prop (parent stores it in ITS OWN local useRef; effect registers on mount, null on cleanup; group persists across preset switches so no re-register). Result: eslint 0 errors 0 warnings with ZERO new eslint-disables.
- Verification: `bunx eslint` on all 6 files → 0 errors, 0 warnings (after the redesign above). git diff reviewed hunk-by-hunk for every file: surgical, no whole-file rewrites, ZERO GLSL lines touched (explicit diff scan for uniform/varying/attribute/gl_/snoise lines → only my own comment mentions curlNoise). Import resolution: @/lib/use-webgl exists under the `@/* → ./src/*` alias; type-only import into three-d-section verified erased (import type). Dev server (hot-reload, never restarted): `GET / 200` (compiles hero-console + three-d-section + use-webgl) and `GET /services/websites 200` (compiles three-d-section's tree) with `✓ Compiled` and ZERO new error lines in dev.log mentioning my files; lazy leaf chunks (hero-canvas/console-scene/capability-scene) parse-verified via eslint's full TS parser (0 problems). node scripts/check-i18n-parity.js → exit 0, 635/635 keys (no new i18n keys needed — cursor label comes from the engine's existing common.cursor.rotate). No tsc/build/browser/DB writes; dev server untouched; git scope = exactly my 5 files + new src/lib/use-webgl.ts.

Stage Summary:
- All 5 fix-list items DONE: capability mouse parallax REVIVED (state.pointer NDC read replaces the dead rect math — the documented "blob+satellites+halo drift together" hover response now actually works); console drag IMPLEMENTED (throttled + pointer-capture + clamped pitch ±0.5 rad + drift-pause + grab/grabbing cursor + ContextLossGuard — the "اسحب للتدوير 360°" affordance is now truthful, and the whole scene box is a drag surface, not just node hits); WebGL gate hardened (new memoized rAF-deferred context-creation probe hook; blocklisted GPUs get the SVG fallback); hero mobile tier shipped (coarse-pointer/<768px → 1800 particles + dpr ≤1.5, reactive without canvas re-creation, desktop byte-identical) + silk plane widened to 80 for 32:9 ultrawides; three-d-section is an accessible interactive visual (role="img" + real focus ring + full arrow-key rotation path through a React 19 imperative handle verified through the dynamic/lazy ref plumbing).
- Design decisions worth keeping: (1) register-callback-instead-of-ref-prop pattern for R3F group sharing across the Canvas boundary — keeps react-compiler quiet in BOTH components with zero disables (reusable next time an R3F scene needs an external mutation channel); (2) data-cursor-label intentionally NOT moved with data-cursor="rotate" onto #hero-console-scene — the engine's common.cursor.rotate fallback renders the correct bilingual label, whereas the old t('placeholder') label would have been the input placeholder text; (3) console drag throttling/clamp constants mirror capability-scene (16ms / 0.01 rad/px) at 0.008 rad/px + MAX_TILT 0.5 rad for the lighter scene.
- Deferred / notes for coordinator: (1) hero-canvas.tsx + capability-scene.tsx still carry their own inline rAF WebGL probes — probeWebGL() is exported and memoized so they can adopt it in a later round (deliberately not swapped now to keep the desktop path byte-identical per the fix spec); (2) keyboard nudge renders only while the capability frameloop is active (offscreen = paused) — inherent to the frameloop gating, no user-reachable path focuses an offscreen scene; (3) three-d-section keeps role="img" (per the fix spec) — semantically the frame is a static image with a bonus keyboard affordance; a future round could revisit role="application"/figure semantics if AT feedback warrants; (4) eslint currently 0/0 across all six files — the register-callback pattern is load-bearing for that, don't "simplify" it back to ref props.

Fix is live-possible now.
- FIX 2 (console drag, L1-C/L1-D P2) VERIFIED: wrapper-div-level onPointerDown/Move/Up/Cancel (whole 280px box is a drag surface); 16ms throttle early-returns BEFORE `lastPointerTs.current = now` AND before `last.current.x/y` update (delta accumulation correct); yaw free `+= dx·0.008`, pitch `THREE.MathUtils.clamp(rot.x + dy·0.008, ±MAX_TILT=0.5)`; `setPointerCapture?.()` optional-chained in down, `hasPointerCapture?.()`-guarded release in endDrag (up AND cancel both call endDrag → flag + state cleared); drift pauses while dragging (`!dragging` in the drift condition) AND drift tilt clamped to the same ±0.5 (old unbounded 0.02/s drift would have flipped the console after ~2.6 min); ContextLossGuard added (byte-mirror of the other two scenes'); cursor truthfully `dragging ? 'grabbing' : 'grab'`; rotation mutates the group published via registerGroup callback (Nodes registers in a [registerGroup]-dep effect with null cleanup — react-compiler clean, eslint 0/0 confirms).
- FIX 3 (WebGL probe gate) VERIFIED: use-webgl.ts probes webgl2 → webgl inside try/catch, memoized in module-level `cached`, SSR path returns false WITHOUT pinning the cache; hook defers probe to rAF (matches hero-canvas's proven glAvailable pattern; setState happens inside the rAF callback, not the effect body — set-state-in-effect rule satisfied; note: it uses useState+rAF rather than useSyncExternalStore, which is correct for a one-shot monotonic value). Branch trace of `showSvg = mounted && (isMobile || reduced || !webglSupported)`: desktop+WebGL → canvas ✓; <768px → SVG ✓; blocklisted GPU (API present, creation fails) → SVG ✓ (old `in window` check greps to zero — only comments reference it); reduced → SVG ✓. Flash trace: `mounted` requires a preset click; the probe resolves at the first rAF after page load (long before any click), and even the theoretical sub-frame click would render SVG→WebGL (safe direction) — no desktop SVG flash.
- FIX 4 (mobile tier) VERIFIED: constants 4500/1800; useMobileTier is the exact use-reduced-motion.ts pattern (module MQL singleton for '(max-width: 767px), (pointer: coarse)' — comma=OR — subscribe/getSnapshot/getServerSnapshot=false); dpr swaps [1,1.5]↔[1,2]; `<Particles key={particleCount}>` remounts so the lazy-useState buffers resize; desktop path byte-equivalent (4500 + dpr [1,2] + constant key). Verified against installed R3F 9.7.0 source that the dpr prop change does NOT recreate the canvas: CanvasImpl's dependency-less useIsomorphicLayoutEffect re-runs `root.configure({...dpr...})` every render; configure's renderer setup is `if (!state.gl)` (one time) and dpr goes through `state.setDpr` → `gl.setPixelRatio` in place.
- FIX 5 (three-d-section a11y) VERIFIED: role="img" + aria-label + `tabIndex={!reduced ? 0 : undefined}` + focus-visible ring (ring-offset-elyra-dark — compiles, unlike the old elyra-deep); Arrow keys → `nudge(±16,0)/(0,±16)`; preventDefault placed AFTER the switch (default case returns early) so only arrow keys are swallowed — no collateral scroll suppression; `sceneRef.current?.nudge` null-guard no-ops before the lazy scene mounts (arrows scroll normally then — correct). Type-only import (`import type { CapabilitySceneHandle}`) verified erased → lazy chunk boundary intact. Ref-plumbing mechanism RE-VERIFIED against installed sources (citations): next 16.1.3 lazy-dynamic/loadable.js renders `<Lazy {...props} />` with ref included in the spread; react 19.2.3 jsx-runtime keeps `ref` IN props (only `key` is stripped); react-dom-client beginWork `case 16` (LazyComponent) passes `workInProgress.pendingProps` — ref included — to the resolved function component via updateFunctionComponent. nudge() applies the SAME ±0.01 rad/px mapping as the pointer drag (capability-scene lines 493-494) to the SAME spinner group. Services pages unaffected: imperativeHandleEffect in react-dom no-ops when ref is undefined (verified in react-dom-client source), and three-d-section is the ONLY CapabilityScene consumer.
- Coordinator drag-postponement fix VERIFIED (hero-console.tsx:99-148 + console-scene.tsx:499-540): (a) first-intent-wins holds — each postponement re-assigns navTimer.current (`window.setTimeout(navigate, 400)`), so `if (navTimer.current) return` blocks later intents while postponing; (b) unmount cleanup (hero-console:110-115) clears the CURRENT handle — the reschedule is synchronous inside the fired callback, so the handle is never stale; (c) drag-forever = an infinite but strictly single-timer chain (one live timeout at a time — no accumulation/leak) that dies at unmount; (d) after release the pending ≤400ms timer fires with dragActiveRef=false → router.push — nav lands within ~400ms; (e) reduced path (250ms) unaffected — reduced users get the SVG diagram, dragActiveRef can never be set; (f) no stale closure — `navigate` closes over qs/router captured at intent time (correct semantics: the intent's own params; goToContact is rebuilt on router/reduced change); (g) ordering confirmed — onPointerDown calls `onDragStateChange?.(true)` BEFORE `e.currentTarget.setPointerCapture?.(...)` (a throwing capture still leaves the flag set, and a failed capture still delivers pointerup → endDrag), and endDrag (both up+cancel) reports false. reportDragState is a stable useCallback mutating only a local ref — react-compiler clean.
- Cursor affordance: `data-cursor={showSvg ? undefined : 'rotate'}` now sits on the hit-testable #hero-console-scene wrapper; verified the custom-cursor engine resolves the label from common.cursor.rotate ("اسحب للتدوير 360°"/"Drag to rotate 360°" — present in BOTH catalogs) when no explicit data-cursor-label exists; the dead pointer-events-none chip attrs are gone.
- Gates: `bunx eslint` on all 6 files → exit 0, ZERO output (0 errors, 0 warnings — react-compiler at warn level included). i18n parity script → 635/635 green. No tsc/build/browser/dev-server touched (analyst contract).
- Regression trace — dragActiveRef strand: preset switch does NOT unmount ConsoleScene (unkeyed `<ConsoleScene preset={selected}>` — same position/type, group persists, pointer capture retained by the wrapper → no strand, drag continues seamlessly). HeroConsole unmount strands nothing (navTimer cleared; dragActiveRef dies with the component). The ONE real strand path: `showSvg` flipping false→true MID-DRAG unmounts ConsoleScene (React swaps the ternary branch → fragment subtree removed; no pointerup fires on a removed element) → dragActiveRef orphaned true → pending navigation postponed forever + all later intents blocked by first-intent-wins until reload. Reachable only via viewport resized across the 768px boundary or an OS reduced-motion toggle while actively dragging — freak probability, but real (reported P3 with one-line fix). webglSupported cannot flip true→false (one-shot, resolves before interaction is possible).

Stage Summary:
VERIFIED FIXES:
- ✓ FIX 1 (L1-D P1) capability-scene parallax revival — dead rect-math removed, state.pointer NDC read with ÷4/0.04, group composition preserved, NaN-safe.
- ✓ FIX 2 (L1-C/L1-D P2) console drag — throttle order, yaw/pitch clamp, guarded capture/release, drift pause + drift clamp, ContextLossGuard, truthful cursor, registerGroup pattern.
- ✓ FIX 3 (L1-C/L1-D P3) WebGL probe gate — memoized creation probe, SSR-safe, rAF-deferred, all four showSvg branches traced, no desktop SVG flash.
- ✓ FIX 4 (L1-D P2+P3) mobile tier — uSES MQL tier, dpr swap in-place (R3F source-verified), keyed remount, desktop byte-equivalent, ZERO GLSL changes (5/5 blocks byte-identical), plane 80×32 with untouched melt/vignette math.
- ✓ FIX 5 (L1-D P3) three-d-section a11y — role="img"/aria/tabIndex/focus-ring/arrow-keys/preventDefault/null-guards/type-only import; ref-through-dynamic mechanism re-verified against installed next 16.1.3 + react-dom 19.2.3.
- ✓ Coordinator's drag-postponement — all seven sub-checks (a)-(g) pass; no timer leak, no double-push, no stale closure, correct ordering.

NEW FINDINGS:
[P3] src/components/home/console-scene.tsx:463-574 (+ hero-console.tsx:281-309) — dragActiveRef can strand true if ConsoleScene unmounts mid-drag
  Evidence: ConsoleScene calls `onDragStateChange?.(true)` in onPointerDown and `?.(false)` only in endDrag (pointerup/pointercancel). hero-console renders `{showSvg ? <ConsoleSvgDiagram/> : <><ConsoleScene …/>…</>}` — when showSvg flips false→true the WebGL branch (and its pointer handlers) unmounts with no cleanup reporting false. Flip sources: isMobile MQL crossing 768px (window resize mid-drag), reduced-motion OS toggle mid-drag (webglSupported cannot flip true→false after resolving).
  Impact: with a pending payoff navigation, the navigate chain postpones forever (dragActiveRef stays true) AND `if (navTimer.current) return` blocks every later intent — the hero's conversion path is dead until page reload. Freak-probability trigger, high-impact result.
  Recommended fix: one line in ConsoleScene — `useEffect(() => () => onDragStateChange?.(false), [onDragStateChange])` (reportDragState is stable, so the cleanup fires exactly on unmount; idempotent when no drag was active).
[P3] src/components/home/hero-canvas.tsx:424 — Particles uPixelRatio is not tier-aware (point-size/render-scale mismatch introduced by the mobile tier)
  Evidence: `uPixelRatio: { value: Math.min(typeof window !== 'undefined' ? window.devicePixelRatio : 1, 2) }` (captured once per mount, memo deps []), while the mobile tier clamps the RENDERER to `dpr={[1,1.5]}`. On a ≥2×-DPR phone: renderer 1.5 vs uniform 2 → gl_PointSize scales 2/1.5 ≈ 1.33× larger than intended relative to render scale (pre-tier code was consistent at dpr 2 / uniform 2).
  Impact: cosmetic only — mobile-tier particle sprites render ~33% oversize relative to the scene on retina phones; no correctness/perf effect (perf goal still achieved via count 1800 + dpr 1.5).
  Recommended fix: clamp the uniform with the tier — e.g. pass `dprMax` as a prop to Particles (`uPixelRatio: { value: Math.min(window.devicePixelRatio, dprMax) }`); the existing key remount on tier flip already re-runs the memo.
[P3] worklog.md:1664 (EOF) — coordinator's LOOP-2 E2E verification notes were never appended
  Evidence: the worklog's last entry is agent 2-b's fix report; the drag-postponement fix (dragActiveRef/reportDragState/navigate closure) exists only in code + inline comments, and no E2E record of it exists anywhere in the log.
  Impact: audit-trail gap only — LOOP 2 has no record of the coordinator's browser verification or the rationale/400ms choice; future rounds auditing "who changed hero-console's goToContact" will find no provenance.
  Recommended fix: coordinator appends its E2E note (routes walked, the drag-postponement observation, gates) when committing LOOP 2.

VERDICT: 3 issues remain (all P3 — one real edge-case logic gap with a one-line fix, one cosmetic mobile-tier nuance, one process/audit-trail gap). All six fixes verified substantively correct with zero regressions found in the diff/fresh-eyes sweep; eslint 0/0 across all six files; GLSL byte-identical; no timer leaks; desktop paths byte-equivalent.

Shipped the semantically identical NON-CAPTURING form `icon(?:$|/)`. Task ID: R8.1
Agent: main
Task: Fix the three issues reported on the Preview Panel — (1) mouse cursor stuck in the top-left corner, (2) the homepage "Assembly" build animation not appearing when entering the site, (3) the before/after page not displaying well.

Work Log:
- CURSOR ROOT CAUSE (reproduced live): the engine's write-guard compared `Math.abs(pos - NaN) > EPSILON` — NaN compares false against everything, so the very first transform write never happened and the layers stayed parked at (0,0) forever while the native cursor was already hidden. Reproduced by patching MediaQueryList.prototype (pointer:fine→true) + locale-switch re-render + CDP mouse moves: dot revealed (is-hidden removed) but transform stayed empty. The R8 "invisible cursor" fix had made this broken engine *visible* at the corner.
- Cursor fix (custom-cursor.tsx): (a) NaN guards (`Number.isNaN(dotWX) || …`) on both write conditions; (b) defense-in-depth — `elyra-cursor-active` (cursor:none) is now added only at the FIRST proven pointermove, so an environment that never delivers pointer events keeps a working native cursor. Verified live: dot exactly at (400,250) after move, then dot(792,446)/ring(603,351) lerping toward (900,500) — correct fast/slow lerp behavior, EN locale too.
- BUILD ANIMATION diagnosis: agent-browser headless reports pointer:fine=false (cursor never mounts there — why previous rounds missed the engine bug). Found Arabic `find --name` clicks fail SILENTLY (report success, click nothing) and dev-mode client navigations take ~2.5s — both produced false test readings; switched to ref/DOM clicks with ≥5s waits. Verified: fresh visit replays intro+build; repeat visit replays from paint; link navigation remounts and replays; browser BACK restores cached DOM with animations parked (the real gap matching the user's report).
- BUILD ANIMATION fixes: (a) R8.1 attention gate — pre-paint layout script arms `data-page-wait` when document.hidden at load (background tab / collapsed preview panel); CSS parks every entrance animation until first visibilitychange→visible (wiring verified: paused↔running). (b) Choreography stretched ~1.75× so the sequence is catchable: scan sweep 1.25s→2.2s (delay 0.3→0.35), content delays retuned (kicker .85, h1 1.0/1.22/1.44, subtitle 1.55, CTAs/rail 1.8, console 2.05), power-on 2.6, sheen 2.7/0.95s, blueprint rest 2.8; scan beam brightened (peak .38→.5). VLM frame-by-frame confirms fresh-visit sequence (intro → scan visible at 50% with content materializing behind → settled) and repeat-visit replay (CTAs absent at 1.2s, materialized at 2.3s). (c) History-return replay — hero listens to pageshow(persisted) + navigation.currententrychange (hash-guarded, homepage-paths-only) and re-arms every one-shot hero animation via the class-toggle+forced-reflow idiom (.hero-fx-restart, CSS `animation:none !important` reset, no intermediate paint). Verified: browser back to / shows scan mid-flight (top=713px op=1 at T+0.9s) then completing.
- BEFORE/AFTER page diagnosis: at 768px (typical preview-panel width) the md:grid-cols-2 layout gave each dense mockup only ~340px — VLM rated 4/10 ("unreadable, squished"). Fix (work-grid.tsx): single column up to lg (1024px). Fix (before-after.tsx): aspect-[4/3] below sm for taller phone frames (16/10 from sm up). VLM re-rate after fix: 8/10 — mockups legible, sliders usable, single column praised. Mobile 390px: no horizontal overflow, 4:3 card legible.
- Test-infra lesson recorded: Arabic accessible-name locators + short waits produce false negatives in this environment; ref/DOM clicks with generous waits are the reliable pattern.

Stage Summary:
- All three reported issues fixed and browser-verified end-to-end: cursor now tracks the pointer (dot+ring, correct lerp, blend inverts over light/dark); the Assembly build sequence now plays in EVERY entry scenario (fresh visit, repeat visit, link navigation, browser back/forward, hidden-tab load) and runs ~3.65s so it can actually be caught; the before/after page renders full-width single-column mockups at panel/tablet widths (4/10→8/10) with taller 4:3 frames on mobile.
- Gates at close: tsc=0, eslint=0, all routes 200, zero browser page errors. Files touched: custom-cursor.tsx, hero.tsx, hero-console.tsx, kinetic-heading.tsx, globals.css, [locale]/layout.tsx, work-grid.tsx, before-after.tsx.

- Read worklog tail (LOOP-6 closure: zero open findings, 13-item ACCEPTED-TRADEOFF LEDGER) + /tmp/stitch-sdk-README.md (generate/edit/variants API, variantOptions: variantCount 1-5, creativeRange REFINE/EXPLORE/REIMAGINE, aspects LAYOUT/COLOR_SCHEME/IMAGES/TEXT_FONT/TEXT_CONTENT; DeviceType MOBILE/DESKTOP/TABLET/AGNOSTIC; modelId GEMINI_3_PRO/GEMINI_3_FLASH; DesignSystem create/update/apply).
- Ran 20+ web searches + ~18 full-page extractions. PRIMARY SOURCES: (1) Official Stitch Prompt Guide by Stitch PM Vincent Nallatamby (discuss.ai.google.dev/t/stitch-prompt-guide/83844, 91 replies/265k views) — vibe adjectives, screen-by-screen refinement, 1-2 changes per prompt, image targeting by content name, "Switch all product copy and button text to X", plain-language-start-then-complexify; community case (tempo, factory dashboard): multi-change prompts break layouts — decompose into structure→filters→details prompts, never mix layout+component changes, save screenshots each step; >5000-char prompts omit components (Hoang/Cristi: 5-7k chars ≈60% there). (2) Official google-labs-code/stitch-skills repo — generate-design SKILL.md (Prompt Enhancement Pipeline; screen template = purpose + PLATFORM + numbered PAGE STRUCTURE; "No theme leakage": NO hex/fonts in generation prompts when a project design system exists, hex OK in edit prompts; content-first: describe what each section CONTAINS, not how it looks; "Do NOT re-generate from scratch unless the fundamental layout is wrong"); prompt-keywords.md + design-mappings.md (vague→professional terminology table, adjective palettes, shape/depth vocabulary); enhance-prompt skill (DESIGN SYSTEM (REQUIRED) block + Page Structure output format, color format "Descriptive Name (#hex) for functional role"); manage-design-system SKILL + tool-schema.md (update_design_system schema: colorMode/headlineFont/bodyFont/labelFont enums INTER/ROBOTO/…/NOTO_SANS — NO Arabic font in confirmed enum; roundness ROUND_FOUR/EIGHT/TWELVE/FULL; customColor seed; colorVariant FIDELITY/TONAL/VIBRANT/EXPRESSIVE/CONTENT/MONOCHROME/FRUIT_SALAD/RAINBOW; overridePrimary/Secondary/Tertiary/Neutral; upload DESIGN.md → create_design_system_from_design_md → tokens live at project level; apply_design_system accepts ONLY id+sourceScreen); stitch-loop skill (baton loop, .stitch/metadata.json persistence, screenshot URL =w{width} for full-res); extract-design-md react-tailwind reference (extraction from tailwind.config/globals.css @theme/next/font variables — directly applicable to Elyra's Tailwind 4 codebase). (3) Official Google blogs: "From idea to app: Introducing Stitch" (May 20 2025: NL/image gen, variants, Paste to Figma, code export); "Introducing vibe design with Stitch" (Mar 18 2026, Rustin Banks: start from business objective + user feeling + inspiration examples; URL design-system extraction; DESIGN.md import/export; voice critique); DESIGN.md open-sourced (Apr 21 2026: agents "know exactly what a color is for", WCAG validation). (4) Case studies: justinmckelvey.com MCP review (closed-loop Claude Code workflow design→implement→refine = 3-5x speedup; HTML/Tailwind/Vue/Angular/Flutter/SwiftUI export; 350 gens/mo free; tokens expire 90d) + honest review (weaknesses: precision work, brand-specific design systems "look AI-generated" vs tailored, 350-cap — mitigation: visual editor tweaks instead of re-prompting); Medium devsecops-ai MCP article (Cloudflare-blocked; snippets: agent reads designs/component structure/color tokens directly); designproject.io Qualcomm 404 test (Apr 2026: design.md = "prompt-conditioner, not a contract"; round 3 "created an entirely new design system on the fly"; enforcement via prompting alone = losing game; 20-min budget per screen; Stitch=divergence, Figma/code=convergence); Codecademy tutorial (full crypto-dashboard prompt + refinement prompts; Flash 350/mo standard vs Pro 50/mo experimental); Xinran Ma substack (Instant Prototype, Direct Edit 4 component actions, design system 3 creation paths, import-from-website buggy, canvas-wide agent context); MindStudio (URL extraction 15-45s, conversational global changes, React/AI Studio export); OpenReplay (Zoom-Out-Zoom-In framework + concrete SaaS example; HTML+TailwindCSS export); process-topixels (DESIGN.md must be CONSTRAINTS not descriptions — token rationale + boundaries, brief-first sequence, 8 explicit don'ts, diagnostic loop generate-3-check-deviate; Claude-generated DESIGN.md: "tokens perfect, reasoning layer fabricated"). (5) Failure-mode evidence: discuss.ai.google.dev "Stitch is becoming almost unusable for real design work" (Jun 2026: prompt-adherence failures, unrelated-area edits, silent no-ops, unwanted new pages, resurrected deleted pages, unreliable Ctrl+Z, no multi-section referencing); "Deep Feedback" (Jun 2026: Hebrew partial localization → RTL/mixed-direction weakness; false confirmations "AI trust breakdown"); "degraded design quality after update" (Apr 2026 + OFFICIAL mitigation from Google CM RISHABH_CHAUHAN: design.md sets hard rules checked before every prompt — e.g. "avoid Material Design, specific hex codes, square corners"; Annotate tool; context overfitting to recent prompts → separate projects); Josh Canale LinkedIn (prompt-quality ceiling: stronger prompt still gave similar layouts); feedback threads (1-image-upload limit, batch edits return fewer pages, "starts creative then needs granular micro-prompts", multi-screen prompt 9→6). (6) Device types: DESKTOP for web landing pages (drlee.io; creative-director skill "DESKTOP unless specified"); mobile version as separate screens on same canvas ("Make a mobile version of these UI screens"); AGNOSTIC in SDK enum; GEMINI_3_PRO for best quality.
- Synthesized the full playbook below; wrote ZERO project files (research-only; this worklog entry is the only append).

Stage Summary:
- TOP 5 PROMPT-ENGINEERING RULES: (1) Vibe first, concrete sensory language ("quiet-luxury architecture studio", NOT "modern/high-end") + business objective + anti-vibe; abstract words produce generic AI slop. (2) Structure > styling: numbered PAGE STRUCTURE (Header/Hero/Content/Footer), content-first ("what each section contains"), UI/UX keywords (sticky nav, card grid, primary CTA), PLATFORM line (Web, Desktop-first). (3) ONE change per edit prompt, never mix layout+component changes, keep prompts <2000 words (>5k chars = dropped components), hex codes only in EDITS (never in generation when a project design system exists — no theme leakage). (4) Edit when the layout is fundamentally right; variants (creativeRange EXPLORE = sweet spot, aspect-targeted LAYOUT/COLOR_SCHEME/…) for exploration; regenerate/new-project when 2 consecutive edits fail (context overfitting). (5) Never trust, always verify: download HTML+screenshot after every call (agent claims success falsely); persist screen IDs + every good state.
- DESIGN-SYSTEM STRATEGY: extract DESIGN.md from Elyra's Tailwind 4 @theme + next/font variables → YAML frontmatter (color tokens, type scale, radius, spacing) + constraint-first body (brief → tokens with intent+boundary → typography decision logic → component logic → 8 don'ts) → upload + create_design_system_from_design_md → apply at project level so generations need no theme tokens. Font enum lacks Cairo → put Cairo/Inter/JetBrains in designMd markdown + verify in exported HTML. Diagnostic loop: generate 3 screens → check deviations → add constraints → repeat.
- WORKFLOW RECOMMENDATION (Elyra, Arabic RTL agency): create project → seed DESIGN.md from codebase → generate sections one-by-one DESKTOP/GEMINI_3_PRO with content-first Arabic prompts incl. explicit RTL line ("navigation starts at the right edge") → verify each (screenshot + HTML dir/font grep) → single-aspect edits → EXPLORE variants for layout exploration → cherry-pick → implement by hand in Next.js/Tailwind (Stitch = divergence, our zero-findings codebase = convergence); RTL fallback = generate LTR structure and mirror in code (our codebase already handles RTL natively); specify Western numerals for stats (matches Elyra convention); 350-gen/mo quota discipline (variants batch up to 5 directions per call).
- SOURCES: discuss.ai.google.dev Stitch Prompt Guide (/t/stitch-prompt-guide/83844); google-labs-code/stitch-skills GitHub (generate-design, manage-design-system, enhance-prompt, stitch-loop, extract-design-md SKILLs); developers.googleblog.com "From idea to app: Introducing Stitch" (May 20 2025); blog.google "Introducing vibe design with Stitch" (Mar 18 2026); blog.google "Stitch app's DESIGN.md format is now open-source" (Apr 21 2026); justinmckelvey.com/blog/google-stitch-mcp + /google-stitch-review (May 31 2026); medium.com/devsecops-ai "How Google Stitch + Claude Code's MCP integration changed the way I build products" (Apr 8 2026, snippets); designproject.io/blog/google-stitch-review (Apr 15 2026); codecademy.com Stitch tutorial; designwithai.substack.com Stitch upgrades walkthrough (Mar 20 2026); mindstudio.ai design-system + DESIGN.md articles (Mar 2026); blog.openreplay.com/prompt-ui-google-stitch (May 15 2026); processtopixels.substack.com DESIGN.md article (May 5 2026); udhaykumarbala/stitch-creative-director SKILL.md; discuss.ai.google.dev threads: known-issues/83848, feedback-competitors/87448, deep-feedback/172927, degraded-quality/137777, almost-unusable/170296, arabic-rtl/89158 (AI Studio).

SO-1 PROMPT (websites hero — same skeleton for automation with motif swap):
"DEVICE: DESKTOP. Hero concept exploration for the Custom Websites service page of a premium Arabic-first digital agency. Apply the project design system 'Elyra — إيليرا' (premium, editorial, cinematic, calm confidence, engineered). Layout-structure exploration only — all copy is placeholder Arabic we will localize; colors/fonts come from the design system. VIBE: an Apple keynote opening frame crossed with a Swiss editorial poster — composed, precise, engineered depth, one focal glow. ANTI-VIBE: playful, neon cyberpunk, corporate flat, generic SaaS, blue-purple gradients, glassmorphism on light, stock-photo collage. DIRECTION: Arabic RTL layout — navigation starts at the right edge, headlines anchor right, primary CTA at the left end of its row; Western numerals (0-9) in any Arabic text; no English body copy. PAGE STRUCTURE: 1. RTL nav bar: brand mark right, four links flowing left, one pill CTA far left. 2. Small tracked kicker label, then a two-line display headline (second line is the accent phrase), then one sentence describing bespoke website design. 3. SIGNATURE ZONE (exploration focus — pick one): (a) floating cluster of overlapping device frames — laptop, tablet, phone — tilted in space around one glowing centerpiece webpage; (b) an abstract webpage blueprint under construction — wireframe blocks, hairline measurement marks, corner brackets, one block materializing in full color; (c) monumental typographic scale play with a single oversized letterform cropped by the viewport edge. 4. One primary pill button + one quiet text link beneath. 5. A hairline grid or faint blueprint underlay giving the dark space structure." — automation variant: signature zone = (a) constellation of small app/tool nodes connected by curved flow lines converging into one hub node, pulses traveling along the lines; (b) a vertical waterfall of document cards flowing between two system columns; (c) an isometric circuit board of business tools. Success criteria: RTL plausibility (headline anchored right, screenshot + HTML dir/font grep), clear kicker<h1<subtitle<CTA hierarchy, exactly one primary action, brand fit (pill CTA, hairlines, calm depth — no neon/purple), composition clearly distinct from the sibling service page's hero, zero English body copy in the frame. Reject (no regenerate-from-scratch) if layout fundamentally wrong after ≤2 single-aspect edits.
SO-2 PROMPT (about story): same system/RTL/vibe block + "editorial storytelling layout for an agency's About page — the emotional core of the site. VIBE: a museum exhibition catalog crossed with a long-form magazine profile — generous whitespace, slow confident rhythm. ANTI-VIBE: resume/CV layout, team-slideshow template, corporate org chart. PAGE STRUCTURE: 1. Editorial opening: kicker + two-line display headline ('The Story of…') + one-sentence standfirst. 2. Narrative block: the founding question set as a LARGE pull-quote breaking the grid, followed by a two-paragraph answer column with a drop-cap-like opening. 3. Milestone timeline: horizontal rail with 4 year-marked nodes (2019→2026), each a one-line caption; the rail thins toward the past. 4. Manifesto strip: the three values as full-bleed editorial panels — one Arabic word each at display size with a one-line caption. 5. Numbers band + team row exist already (out of scope)." Success: pull-quote dominates the fold, timeline reads right-to-left with years as Western numerals, values panels feel editorial not card-grid.
SO-3 PROMPT (contact quote card): "EXPLORE, 3 variants. Redesign one social-proof card for a contact page: a five-star rating row, one short client quote, client name/role/company, and two metric badges (+140%, +85%). VIBE: editorial pull-quote — the quote should feel hand-set, not boxed. ANTI-VIBE: testimonial-carousel card, nested rounded boxes, badge soup." Success: quote typographically dominant, metrics read as a footnote strip, no more than one border level.

- DEFECT FOUND & FIXED during E2E (missed by G4-5's network-request methodology): scene images inside the before/after slider's clip-path region never lazy-load — Chromium's lazy-load intersection does not fire for clip-concealed images and does NOT re-evaluate on clip-path changes (verified: image in-viewport 2.5s → still naturalWidth 0; slider drag to 94% → still unloaded; eager force → loads instantly). Fix: removed loading="lazy" from all 7 scene-image sites in before-after.tsx (19 tiny local webps ≈300KB total — eager is both reliable and cheap; miniature-screenshot semantics). Post-fix browser verification: 19/19 loaded at 1280 AND 390. Documented in-code at the store-hero site.
- Final gates: tsc=0 · eslint=0 · parity 736 GREEN · dev server 200 throughout (restarted once after the documented OOM kill).
